=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a positive integer N, and an integer A_{x,y,z} for each triple of integers (x, y, z) such that 1 \leq x, y, z \leq N.
You will be given Q queries in the following format, which must be processed in order.
For the i-th query (1 \leq i \leq Q), you are given a tuple of integers (Lx_i, Rx_i, Ly_i, Ry_i, Lz_i, Rz_i) such that 1 \leq Lx_i \leq Rx_i \leq N, 1 \leq Ly_i \leq Ry_i \leq N, and 1 \leq Lz_i \leq Rz_i \leq N. Find:
\displaystyle{\sum_{x=Lx_i}^{Rx_i} \sum_{y=Ly_i}^{Ry_i} \sum_{z=Lz_i}^{Rz_i} A_{x,y,z}}.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_{1,1,1} A_{1,1,2} \ldots A_{1,1,N}
A_{1,2,1} A_{1,2,2} \ldots A_{1,2,N}
\vdots
A_{1,N,1} A_{1,N,2} \ldots A_{1,N,N}
A_{2,1,1} A_{2,1,2} \ldots A_{2,1,N}
A_{2,2,1} A_{2,2,2} \ldots A_{2,2,N}
\vdots
A_{2,N,1} A_{2,N,2} \ldots A_{2,N,N}
\vdots
A_{N,1,1} A_{N,1,2} \ldots A_{N,1,N}
A_{N,2,1} A_{N,2,2} \ldots A_{N,2,N}
\vdots
A_{N,N,1} A_{N,N,2} \ldots A_{N,N,N}
Q
Lx_1 Rx_1 Ly_1 Ry_1 Lz_1 Rz_1
Lx_2 Rx_2 Ly_2 Ry_2 Lz_2 Rz_2
\vdots
Lx_Q Rx_Q Ly_Q Ry_Q Lz_Q Rz_Q

Output

Print Q lines.
The i-th line should contain the answer to the i-th query.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 100
- 1 \leq Q \leq 2 \times 10^{5}
- 0 \leq A_{x,y,z} \leq 999 (1 \leq x, y, z \leq N)
- 1 \leq Lx_i \leq Rx_i \leq N (1 \leq i \leq Q)
- 1 \leq Ly_i \leq Ry_i \leq N (1 \leq i \leq Q)
- 1 \leq Lz_i \leq Rz_i \leq N (1 \leq i \leq Q)
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

2
1 2
3 4
5 6
7 8
2
1 2 2 2 1 1
2 2 1 2 1 2

Sample Output 1

10
26

For the 1st query, the sought value is A_{1,2,1} + A_{2,2,1} = 3 + 7 = 10. Thus, print 10.
For the 2nd query, the sought value is A_{2,1,1} + A_{2,1,2} + A_{2,2,1} + A_{2,2,2} = 5 + 6 + 7 + 8 = 26. Thus, print 26.

1 3 2 3 3 3

1 3 1 1 1 1
2 3 2 3 2 3
1 2 1 1 1 2
3 3 2 2 1 3
1 2 2 3 2 3

Sample Output 2

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's write code:

import sys

def solve():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read()
    ndata = len(data)
    pos = 0
    def next_int():
        nonlocal pos
        while pos < ndata and data[pos] <= 32:
            pos += 1
        num = 0
        while pos < ndata and data[pos] > 32:
            num = num * 10 + (data[pos] - 48)
            pos += 1
        return num

    N = next_int()
    stride = N + 1
    st2 = stride * stride
    pref = [0] * (st2 * stride)  # (N+1)^3

    for x in range(1, N + 1):
        xbase = x * st2
        for y in range(1, N + 1):
            idx = xbase + y * stride + 1
            for z in range(1, N + 1):
                a = next_int()
                pref[idx] = (a
                             + pref[idx - st2]
                             + pref[idx - stride]
                             + pref[idx - 1]
                             - pref[idx - st2 - stride]
                             - pref[idx - st2 - 1]
                             - pref[idx - stride - 1]
                             + pref[idx - st2 - stride - 1])
                idx += 1

    Q = next_int()
    out = []
    for _ in range(Q):
        lx = next_int(); rx = next_int()
        ly = next_int(); ry = next_int()
        lz = next_int(); rz = next_int()
        ans = (pref[rx * st2 + ry * stride + rz]
               - pref[(lx - 1) * st2 + ry * stride + rz]
               - pref[rx * st2 + (ly - 1) * stride + rz]
               - pref[rx * st2 + ry * stride + (lz - 1)]
               + pref[(lx - 1) * st2 + (ly - 1) * stride + rz]
               + pref[(lx - 1) * st2 + ry * stride + (lz - 1)]
               + pref[rx * st2 + (ly - 1) * stride + (lz - 1)]
               - pref[(lx - 1) * st2 + (ly - 1) * stride + (lz - 1)])
        out.append(str(ans))
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()

Need verify query formula signs. Let's derive from 3D inclusion-exclusion:
F(x,y,z)=sum_{i<=x,j<=y,k<=z} A[i,j,k].
Sum over x in [lx,rx], y in [ly,ry], z in [lz,rz] =
F(rx,ry,rz)
- F(lx-1,ry,rz)
- F(rx,ly-1,rz)
- F(rx,ry,lz-1)
+ F(lx-1,ly-1,rz)

The generator has one `for` loop and yield; but `next(it)` per int. It might be simpler:
def ints():
    num = 0
    in_num = False
    for c in data:
        if c > 32:
            num = num * 10 + c - 48
            in_num = True
        elif in_num:
            yield num
            num = 0
            in_num = False
    if in_num:
        yield num
it = ints()
N = next(it)
...
a = next(it)
This avoids nonlocal and while loops; but `next(it)` resumes generator, and the generator loops over data. It will process all bytes including whitespace.             in_num = True
        elif in_num:
            yield num
            num = 0
            in_num = False
    if in_num:
        yield num

it = ints()
N = next(it)
...
a = next(it)
...
Q = next(it)
...
lx = next(it) ...

Similarly, in prefix loop, localize `p`, `s`, `s2`, `ni`. Let's write final code with local aliases:
def solve():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read()
    ndata = len(data)
    pos = 0
    def next_int():
        nonlocal pos
        while pos < ndata and data[pos] <= 32:
            pos += 1
        num = 0
        while pos < ndata and data[pos] > 32:
            num = num * 10 + data[pos] - 48
            pos += 1
        return num

    N = next_int()
    s = N + 1
    s2 = s * s
    p = [0] * (s2 * s)
    ni = next_int

    for x in range(1, N + 1):
        xb = x * s2
        for y in range(1, N + 1):
            idx = xb + y * s + 1
            for _ in range(1, N + 1):
                a = ni()
                p[idx] = (a
                          + p[idx - s2]
                          + p[idx - s]
                          + p[idx - 1]
                          - p[idx - s2 - s]
                          - p[idx - s2 - 1]
                          - p[idx - s - 1]
                          + p[idx - s2 - s - 1])
                idx += 1

    Q = ni()
    out = []
    append = out.append
    for _ in range(Q):
        lx = ni(); rx = ni()
        ly = ni(); ry = ni()
        lz = ni(); rz = ni()
        ans = (p[rx * s2 + ry * s + rz]
               - p[(lx - 1) * s2 + ry * s + rz]
               - p[rx * s2 + (ly - 1) * s + rz]
               - p[rx * s2 + ry * s + (lz - 1)]
               + p[(lx - 1) * s2 + (ly - 1) * s + rz]
               + p[(lx - 1) * s2 + ry * s + (lz - 1)]
               + p[rx * s2 + (ly - 1) * s + (lz - 1)]
               - p[(lx - 1) * s2 + (ly - 1) * s + (lz - 1)])
        append(str(ans))
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

Need check operator precedence in `num = num * 10 + data[pos] - 48`: Since `+` and `-` same precedence left-to-right, it's `(num*10 + data[pos]) - 48`, same as `num*10 + (data[pos]-48)`. Good. Could write parentheses for clarity.
